~230K tokens/s → a full run would take ~18 min. Let me profile throughput to see if I can speed it up before running experiments.

Ran 1 commandBash ×1
#13 Bashmkdir -p /workspace/scripts && cat > /workspace/scripts/bench.py <<'EOF'
mkdir -p /workspace/scripts && cat > /workspace/scripts/bench.py <<'EOF'
import sys, time, json, torch, torch.nn.functional as F
sys.path.insert(0, sys.argv[1])
import model as M
cfg = json.load(open(sys.argv[1] + "/config.json"))
rows = int(sys.argv[2]) if len(sys.argv) > 2 else 64
body = M.build_model({**cfg, "vocab_size": 8192}).cuda()
body.stack = torch.compile(body.apply_blocks)
ids = torch.randint(0, 8192, (rows, 512), device="cuda")
def step():
    with torch.autocast("cuda", dtype=torch.bfloat16):
        h = body(ids)
        logits = F.linear(h, body.embed.weight)
    loss = F.cross_entropy(logits.float().flatten(0, 1), ids.flatten())
    loss.backward()
for _ in range(3): step()
torch.cuda.synchronize(); t = time.time()
n = 10
for _ in range(n): step()
torch.cuda.synchronize(); dt = (time.time() - t) / n
print(f"rows {rows}: {dt*1000:.1f} ms/step, {rows*512/dt/1e3:.0f}K tok/s, mem {torch.cuda.max_memory_allocated()/1e9:.1f} GB")
EOF

rows 128: 72.4 ms/step, 905K tok/s, mem 29.7 GB

Steady state is ~820K tok/s → a full 250M run takes ~5–6 min. Let me build a helper to spawn experiment variants with config overrides.

Ran 1 commandBash ×1
#15 Bashcat > /workspace/scripts/mk.py <<'EOF'
cat > /workspace/scripts/mk.py <<'EOF'
"""python scripts/mk.py NAME [--from DIR] section.key=value ...  -> exps/NAME (copy of submission with overrides)"""
import json, shutil, sys
from pathlib import Path
args = sys.argv[1:]
name = args.pop(0)
src = Path("submission")
if args and args[0] == "--from":
    args.pop(0); src = Path(args.pop(0))
dst = Path("exps") / name
dst.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
for f in src.glob("*.py"):
    shutil.copy(f, dst / f.name)
cfg = json.loads((src / "config.json").read_text())
for a in args:
    k, v = a.split("=", 1)
    sec, key = k.split(".")
    try:
        v = json.loads(v)
    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        pass
    cfg[sec][key] = v
(dst / "config.json").write_text(json.dumps(cfg, indent=2))
print(dst, json.dumps(cfg))
EOF
cat > /workspace/scripts/run.sh <<'EOF'
#!/bin/bash
# run.sh NAME TOKENS SEED  -> trains exps/NAME, output runs/NAME_sSEED
name=$1; tokens=${2:-250e6}; seed=${3:-0}
cd /workspace
nohup python -m lmenv.run train --submission exps/$name --out runs/${name}_s$seed --seed $seed --tokens $tokens > runs/${name}_s$seed.out 2>&1 &
EOF
chmod +x /workspace/scripts/run.sh; echo ok
ok

[stdout]
ok

The FLOP counter's module hooks choke on a parameter view passed as a module argument. I'll pass the application index instead and keep the temperature inside the attention module.

Ran 1 commandBash ×1
#54 Bashcd /workspace/submission && python - <<'EOF'
cd /workspace/submission && python - <<'EOF'
s = open('model.py').read()
s = s.replace("""    def __init__(self, cfg: ModelConfig):
        super().__init__()
        self.n_heads, self.n_kv, self.hd = cfg.n_heads, cfg.n_kv_heads, cfg.head_dim""",
"""    def __init__(self, cfg: ModelConfig):
        super().__init__()
        self.n_heads, self.n_kv, self.hd = cfg.n_heads, cfg.n_kv_heads, cfg.head_dim
        if cfg.attn_temp > 0:  # per-application, per-head scale on the normalised queries
            self.temp = nn.Parameter(torch.full((cfg.n_apps, cfg.n_heads), cfg.attn_temp))
        else:
            self.temp = None""")
s = s.replace("""    def forward(self, x, cos, sin, ve=None, temp=None):""", """    def forward(self, x, cos, sin, ve=None, app=0):""")

            left = max(0.0, 1 - progress) / decay  # 1 -> 0 over the decay phase

k3 (attention-heavier) is incompatible with value-embeds (kv width > 512); dropping it. Now the NaN safety net:

2 tool callsRead ×1, Bash ×1
#84 Read/workspace/submission/train.py
95	    rows, accumulation = cfg["micro_batch"], cfg["accumulation"]
96	    params = list(body.parameters())
97	    update = 0
98	    model.train()
99	    while not budget.exhausted:
100	        f = lr_factor(budget.fraction_used)
101	        for opt in (muon, adam):
102	            for group in opt.param_groups:
103	                group["lr"] = group["base_lr"] * f
104	        # Muon momentum warmup
105	        m_warm = cfg.get("muon_momentum_warmup", 0)
106	        if m_warm:
107	            frac = min(1.0, update / m_warm)
108	            for group in muon.param_groups:
109	                group["momentum"] = (1 - frac) * 0.85 + frac * cfg.get("muon_momentum", 0.95)
110	        seq_len = 512
111	        for start, length in cfg.get("seq_schedule", []):
112	            if budget.fraction_used >= start:
113	                seq_len = length
114	        total = 0.0
115	        for _ in range(accumulation):
116	            batch = ctx.data.sample(rows * 512 // seq_len, seq_len)
117	            with torch.autocast(ctx.device.type, dtype=torch.bfloat16):
118	                logits = model(batch.tokens)
119	            loss = F.cross_entropy(logits.float().flatten(0, 1), batch.targets.flatten())
120	            (loss / accumulation).backward()
121	            total += loss.detach() / accumulation
122	        if cfg.get("clip", 0):
123	            torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(params, cfg["clip"])
124	        muon.step()
125	        adam.step()
126	        muon.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
127	        adam.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
128	        update += 1
129	        if update % cfg["log_every"] == 0:
130	            total = total.item()
131	            if not math.isfinite(total):
132	                raise FloatingPointError(f"loss is {total} at update {update}")
133	            ctx.log({"update": update, "loss": total, "lr_factor": f})

    update, lr_scale = 0, 1.0

    # Safety net: keep the last state with a finite loss; if the loss ever turns
    # non-finite, go back to it with half the learning rate instead of failing.
    def snapshot_state():
        return ({k: v.detach().clone() for k, v in body.state_dict().items()},
                copy.deepcopy(muon.state_dict()), copy.deepcopy(adam.state_dict()))

    def restore_state(state):
        body.load_state_dict(state[0])
        muon.load_state_dict(state[1])
        adam.load_state_dict(state[2])
        for opt in (muon, adam):
            opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)

    good = snapshot_state()
    model.train()
    while not budget.exhausted:
        f = lr_factor(budget.fraction_used) * lr_scale""")

def apply_blocks(self, x, cos, sin):
        cfg = self.cfg
        x0 = x
        x0_prev = F.pad(x0[:, :-1], (0, 0, 1, 0)) if cfg.ve_prev else None
        for i, bi in enumerate(self.schedule):
            block = self.blocks[bi]
            if cfg.x0_mix:
                x = x + self.x0_lambda[i] * x0
            if cfg.app_embed:
                x = x + self.app_emb[i]
            if block.attn is not None and i % cfg.attn_every == 0:
                ve = self.ve_gain[i] * x0[..., :self.ve_gain.shape[1]] if cfg.value_embed else None
                if cfg.ve_prev:
                    ve = ve + self.ve_prev_gain[i] * x0_prev[..., :self.ve_prev_gain.shape[1]]
                a_in = _rms(x) * self.attn_norm[i]
                if cfg.token_shift:
                    a_in = a_in + self.shift_mix[i] * F.pad(a_in[:, :-1], (0, 0, 1, 0))
                x = x + self.attn_gain[i] * block.attn(a_in, cos, sin, ve, i)
            x = x + self.mlp_gain[i] * block.mlp(_rms(x) * self.mlp_norm[i], i)
            if i in cfg.selfcond:
                j = cfg.selfcond.index(i)
                table = self.embed.weight.to(x.dtype)
                p = torch.softmax(F.linear(_rms(x) * self.sc_norm[j], table).float(), dim=-1).to(x.dtype)
                x = x + self.sc_gain[j] * _rms(p @ table)
        return _rms(x) * self.final_norm

    def forward(self, input_ids):
        length = input_ids.shape[1]
        positions = torch.arange(length, device=input_ids.device, dtype=torch.float32)
        angles = torch.outer(positions, self.inv_freq)
        angles = torch.cat((angles, angles), dim=-1)[None, None]
        x = _rms(self.embed(input_ids))
        return self.stack(x, angles.cos(), angles.sin())  # the harness applies the tied output head


def build_model(config: dict) -> LoopedLM:
    """config is config.json plus {"vocab_size": ...} from the harness."""

    return LoopedLM(ModelConfig(vocab_size=config["vocab_size"], **config.get("model", {})))

        cfg, n = self.cfg, self.cfg.streams
        x0 = x
        streams = [x] * n
        j = 0

        def read(j):
            return sum(self.hc_read[j, k] * streams[k] for k in range(n))

        def write(j, out):
            return [sum(self.hc_mix[j, k, m] * streams[m] for m in range(n)) + self.hc_write[j, k] * out
                    for k in range(n)]

        for i, bi in enumerate(self.schedule):
            block = self.blocks[bi]
            if cfg.x0_mix:
                streams[0] = streams[0] + self.x0_lambda[i] * x0
            if cfg.app_embed:

            if cfg.token_shift:
                a_in = a_in + self.shift_mix[i] * F.pad(a_in[:, :-1], (0, 0, 1, 0))

            j += 1
            streams = write(j, self.mlp_gain[i] * block.mlp(_rms(read(j)) * self.mlp_norm[i], i))
            j += 1
        return _rms(sum(streams)) * self.final_norm

cd /workspace; mkdir -p /tmp/presclean && cp submission/*.py submission/config.json /tmp/presclean/; cat submission/model.py | sed -n 1,60p
"""Weight-shared looped transformer with a wide (free) token embedding.

A few unique transformer blocks (RMSNorm pre-norm, causal attention with RoPE
and QK-norm, ReLU^2 or SwiGLU MLP) are applied many times in a fixed schedule,
so the model spends far more compute per token than it has weights. Each
application of a block gets its own cheap per-application parameters
(residual-branch gains, a mix-in of the token embedding x0, and an
application embedding), so the shared weights can behave differently at
different depths. The token embedding (8192 x width) is not counted against
the weight cap, so the width is the maximum allowed, 512.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from dataclasses import dataclass

import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from torch import nn


@dataclass
class ModelConfig:
    vocab_size: int = 8192
    d_model: int = 512
    n_heads: int = 8
    n_kv_heads: int = 8
    head_dim: int = 64
    mlp_hidden: int = 1536
    mlp_act: str = "relu2"  # relu2 | swiglu | gelu
    n_blocks: int = 1  # unique blocks
    n_apps: int = 16  # block applications per forward pass
    pattern: str = "cycle"  # cycle: 0,1,..,n-1,0,1,..  |  repeat: 0,0,..,1,1,..
    rope_theta: float = 10_000.0
    rope_frac: float = 1.0  # fraction of rotary frequency pairs kept (the lowest frequencies are dropped)
    qk_norm: bool = True
    app_embed: bool = True
    x0_mix: bool = True
    embed_std: float = 0.02
    attn_every: int = 1  # attention in every application (1) or every k-th
    value_embed: bool = False  # add a per-application gain times the token embedding to the attention values
    attn_temp: float = 0.0  # >0: learnable per-application, per-head scale on q (init value), after QK-norm
    hidden_gain: bool = False  # per-application gain on the MLP hidden units
    token_shift: bool = False  # per-application, per-channel mix of the previous position into the attention input
    ve_prev: bool = False  # also add a per-application gain times the previous token's embedding to the values
    streams: int = 1  # >1: static hyper-connections (several residual streams with learned read/write/mix)
    selfcond: tuple = ()  # after these applications: soft next-token guess through the embedding table, re-embedded


def _rms(x, eps=1e-6):
    return F.rms_norm(x, (x.shape[-1],), eps=eps)


def _rotate_half(x):
    first, second = x.chunk(2, dim=-1)
    return torch.cat((-second, first), dim=-1)


class Attention(nn.Module):

[stdout]
"""Weight-shared looped transformer with a wide (free) token embedding.

A few unique transformer blocks (RMSNorm pre-norm, causal attention with RoPE
and QK-norm, ReLU^2 or SwiGLU MLP) are applied many times in a fixed schedule,
so the model spends far more compute per token than it has weights. Each
application of a block gets its own cheap per-application parameters
(residual-branch gains, a mix-in of the token embedding x0, and an
application embedding), so the shared weights can behave differently at
different depths. The token embedding (8192 x width) is not counted against
the weight cap, so the width is the maximum allowed, 512.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from dataclasses import dataclass

import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from torch import nn


@dataclass
class ModelConfig:
    vocab_size: int = 8192
    d_model: int = 512
    n_heads: int = 8
    n_kv_heads: int = 8
    head_dim: int = 64
    mlp_hidden: int = 1536
    mlp_act: str = "relu2"  # relu2 | swiglu | gelu
    n_blocks: int = 1  # unique blocks
    n_apps: int = 16  # block applications per forward pass
    pattern: str = "cycle"  # cycle: 0,1,..,n-1,0,1,..  |  repeat: 0,0,..,1,1,..
    rope_theta: float = 10_000.0
    rope_frac: float = 1.0  # fraction of rotary frequency pairs kept (the lowest frequencies are dropped)
    qk_norm: bool = True
    app_embed: bool = True
    x0_mix: bool = True
    embed_std: float = 0.02
    attn_every: int = 1  # attention in every application (1) or every k-th
    value_embed: bool = False  # add a per-application gain times the token embedding to the attention values
    attn_temp: float = 0.0  # >0: learnable per-application, per-head scale on q (init value), after QK-norm
    hidden_gain: bool = False  # per-application gain on the MLP hidden units
    token_shift: bool = False  # per-application, per-channel mix of the previous position into the attention input
    ve_prev: bool = False  # also add a per-application gain times the previous token's embedding to the values
    streams: int = 1  # >1: static hyper-connections (several residual streams with learned read/write/mix)
    selfcond: tuple = ()  # after these applications: soft next-token guess through the embedding table, re-embedded


def _rms(x, eps=1e-6):
    return F.rms_norm(x, (x.shape[-1],), eps=eps)


def _rotate_half(x):
    first, second = x.chunk(2, dim=-1)
    return torch.cat((-second, first), dim=-1)

#93 Write/workspace/submission/model.py
"""Weight-shared looped transformer with a wide (free) token embedding.

One transformer block (RMSNorm pre-norm; causal attention with RoPE, QK-norm
and a learnable per-head temperature; SwiGLU MLP of width 1200) is applied 16
times per token, so the model spends ~118M FLOPs per token with only ~2.98M
weights. The token embedding (8192 x 512) is not counted against the weight
cap, so the width is the maximum allowed, 512.

Each application of the shared block has its own cheap per-application
parameters (vectors of width 512 or smaller, ~3% of the weights), so the
same weights can act differently at different depths:
  * gains on the attention and MLP branch outputs and on their RMSNorm inputs,
  * x0 mixing: a learned multiple of the (normalised) token embedding is added
    to the residual stream before every application (late applications learn
    to subtract it),
  * an application embedding added to the residual stream,
  * value embeddings: a learned multiple of the token embedding is added to
    the attention values,
  * a per-head temperature on the normalised queries (with QK-norm the
    attention logits are otherwise bounded by sqrt(head_dim)),
  * gains on the MLP hidden units,
  * a token shift: a learned per-channel mix of the previous position's
    attention input into the current one (causal).

The harness applies the tied output head: logits = hidden @ embed.weight.T.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from dataclasses import dataclass

import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from torch import nn


@dataclass
class ModelConfig:
    vocab_size: int = 8192
    d_model: int = 512
    n_heads: int = 8
    n_kv_heads: int = 8
    head_dim: int = 64
    mlp_hidden: int = 1200
    mlp_act: str = "swiglu"  # swiglu | relu2 | gelu
    n_blocks: int = 1  # unique blocks
    n_apps: int = 16  # block applications per forward pass
    pattern: str = "cycle"  # cycle: 0,1,..,n-1,0,1,..  |  repeat: 0,0,..,1,1,..
    rope_theta: float = 10_000.0
    qk_norm: bool = True
    app_embed: bool = True
    x0_mix: bool = True
    embed_std: float = 0.02
    value_embed: bool = True  # add a per-application gain times the token embedding to the attention values
    attn_temp: float = 2.0  # >0: learnable per-application, per-head scale on q (init value), after QK-norm
    hidden_gain: bool = True  # per-application gain on the MLP hidden units
    token_shift: bool = True  # per-application, per-channel mix of the previous position into the attention input


def _rms(x, eps=1e-6):
    return F.rms_norm(x, (x.shape[-1],), eps=eps)


def _rotate_half(x):
    first, second = x.chunk(2, dim=-1)
    return torch.cat((-second, first), dim=-1)


class Attention(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, cfg: ModelConfig):
        super().__init__()
        self.n_heads, self.n_kv, self.hd = cfg.n_heads, cfg.n_kv_heads, cfg.head_dim
        if cfg.attn_temp > 0:  # per-application, per-head scale on the normalised queries
            self.temp = nn.Parameter(torch.full((cfg.n_apps, cfg.n_heads), cfg.attn_temp))
        else:
            self.temp = None
        self.q = nn.Linear(cfg.d_model, cfg.n_heads * cfg.head_dim, bias=False)
        self.k = nn.Linear(cfg.d_model, cfg.n_kv_heads * cfg.head_dim, bias=False)
        self.v = nn.Linear(cfg.d_model, cfg.n_kv_heads * cfg.head_dim, bias=False)
        self.o = nn.Linear(cfg.n_heads * cfg.head_dim, cfg.d_model, bias=False)
        self.qk_norm = cfg.qk_norm

    def forward(self, x, cos, sin, ve=None, app=0):
        b, l, _ = x.shape
        q = self.q(x).view(b, l, self.n_heads, self.hd).transpose(1, 2)
        k = self.k(x).view(b, l, self.n_kv, self.hd).transpose(1, 2)
        v = self.v(x)
        if ve is not None:
            v = v + ve
        v = v.view(b, l, self.n_kv, self.hd).transpose(1, 2)
        if self.qk_norm:
            q, k = _rms(q), _rms(k)
        if self.temp is not None:
            q = q * self.temp[app][None, :, None, None]
        q, k = q * cos + _rotate_half(q) * sin, k * cos + _rotate_half(k) * sin
        if self.n_kv != self.n_heads:
            rep = self.n_heads // self.n_kv
            k = k.repeat_interleave(rep, dim=1)
            v = v.repeat_interleave(rep, dim=1)
        out = F.scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, is_causal=True)
        return self.o(out.transpose(1, 2).reshape(b, l, self.n_heads * self.hd))


class MLP(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, cfg: ModelConfig):
        super().__init__()
        self.act = cfg.mlp_act
        n_in = 2 if cfg.mlp_act == "swiglu" else 1
        self.fc = nn.Linear(cfg.d_model, n_in * cfg.mlp_hidden, bias=False)
        self.proj = nn.Linear(cfg.mlp_hidden, cfg.d_model, bias=False)
        self.hgain = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(cfg.n_apps, cfg.mlp_hidden)) if cfg.hidden_gain else None

    def forward(self, x, app=0):
        h = self.fc(x)
        if self.act == "swiglu":
            a, g = h.chunk(2, dim=-1)
            h = F.silu(g) * a
        elif self.act == "relu2":
            h = F.relu(h).square()
        else:
            h = F.gelu(h, approximate="tanh")
        if self.hgain is not None:
            h = h * self.hgain[app]
        return self.proj(h)


class Block(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, cfg: ModelConfig):
        super().__init__()
        self.attn = Attention(cfg)
        self.mlp = MLP(cfg)


class LoopedLM(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, cfg: ModelConfig):
        super().__init__()
        self.cfg = cfg
        d = cfg.d_model
        self.embed = nn.Embedding(cfg.vocab_size, d)
        self.blocks = nn.ModuleList(Block(cfg) for _ in range(cfg.n_blocks))
        if cfg.pattern == "cycle":
            self.schedule = [i % cfg.n_blocks for i in range(cfg.n_apps)]
        else:
            per = math.ceil(cfg.n_apps / cfg.n_blocks)
            self.schedule = [min(i // per, cfg.n_blocks - 1) for i in range(cfg.n_apps)]
        A = cfg.n_apps
        # per-application parameters (cheap): branch output gains, norm gains, x0 mixing, application embedding
        self.attn_gain = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(A, d))
        self.mlp_gain = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(A, d))
        self.attn_norm = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(A, d))
        self.mlp_norm = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(A, d))
        if cfg.x0_mix:
            self.x0_lambda = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(A, d))
        if cfg.app_embed:
            self.app_emb = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(A, d))
        if cfg.token_shift:
            self.shift_mix = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(A, d))
        if cfg.value_embed:
            assert cfg.n_kv_heads * cfg.head_dim <= d, "value embeddings need n_kv_heads * head_dim <= d_model"
            self.ve_gain = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(A, cfg.n_kv_heads * cfg.head_dim))
        self.final_norm = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(d))
        inv_freq = 1.0 / cfg.rope_theta ** (torch.arange(0, cfg.head_dim, 2, dtype=torch.float32) / cfg.head_dim)
        self.register_buffer("inv_freq", inv_freq, persistent=False)

        nn.init.normal_(self.embed.weight, std=cfg.embed_std)
        for block in self.blocks:
            for p in block.parameters():
                if p.dim() == 2 and p is not block.attn.temp and p is not block.mlp.hgain:
                    nn.init.normal_(p, std=1.0 / math.sqrt(p.shape[1]))
            nn.init.zeros_(block.attn.o.weight)
            nn.init.zeros_(block.mlp.proj.weight)
        self.stack = self.apply_blocks  # train.py replaces this with a compiled version

    def apply_blocks(self, x, cos, sin):
        cfg = self.cfg
        x0 = x
        for i, bi in enumerate(self.schedule):
            block = self.blocks[bi]
            if cfg.x0_mix:
                x = x + self.x0_lambda[i] * x0
            if cfg.app_embed:
                x = x + self.app_emb[i]
            ve = self.ve_gain[i] * x0[..., :self.ve_gain.shape[1]] if cfg.value_embed else None
            a_in = _rms(x) * self.attn_norm[i]
            if cfg.token_shift:  # causal: position t mixes in position t-1 (zeros before the start)
                a_in = a_in + self.shift_mix[i] * F.pad(a_in[:, :-1], (0, 0, 1, 0))
            x = x + self.attn_gain[i] * block.attn(a_in, cos, sin, ve, i)
            x = x + self.mlp_gain[i] * block.mlp(_rms(x) * self.mlp_norm[i], i)
        return _rms(x) * self.final_norm

    def forward(self, input_ids):
        length = input_ids.shape[1]
        positions = torch.arange(length, device=input_ids.device, dtype=torch.float32)
        angles = torch.outer(positions, self.inv_freq)
        angles = torch.cat((angles, angles), dim=-1)[None, None]
        x = _rms(self.embed(input_ids))
        return self.stack(x, angles.cos(), angles.sin())  # the harness applies the tied output head


def build_model(config: dict) -> LoopedLM:
    """config is config.json plus {"vocab_size": ...} from the harness."""

    return LoopedLM(ModelConfig(vocab_size=config["vocab_size"], **config.get("model", {})))

"""Weight-shared looped transformer with a wide (free) token embedding.

One transformer block (RMSNorm pre-norm; causal attention with RoPE, QK-norm
and a learnable per-head temperature; SwiGLU MLP of width 1200) is applied 16
times per token, so the model spends ~118M FLOPs per token with only ~2.98M
weights. The token embedding (8192 x 512) is not counted against the weight
cap, so the width is the maximum allowed, 512.

Each application of the shared block has its own cheap per-application
parameters (vectors of width <= 1200, ~3% of the weights), so the same
weights can act differently at different depths:
  * gains on the attention and MLP branch outputs and on their RMSNorm inputs,
  * x0 mixing: a learned multiple of the (normalised) token embedding is added
    to the residual stream before every application (late applications learn
    to subtract it),
  * an application embedding added to the residual stream,
  * value embeddings: a learned multiple of the token embedding is added to
    the attention values,
  * a per-head temperature on the normalised queries (with QK-norm the
    attention logits are otherwise bounded by sqrt(head_dim)),
  * gains on the MLP hidden units,

    attention input into the current one (causal).

The harness applies the tied output head: logits = hidden @ embed.weight.T.

import math
from dataclasses import dataclass

import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from torch import nn


@dataclass
class ModelConfig:
    vocab_size: int = 8192
    d_model: int = 512
    n_heads: int = 8
    n_kv_heads: int = 8
    head_dim: int = 64
    mlp_hidden: int = 1200
    mlp_act: str = "swiglu"  # swiglu | relu2 | gelu
    n_blocks: int = 1  # unique blocks
    n_apps: int = 16  # block applications per forward pass
    pattern: str = "cycle"  # cycle: 0,1,..,n-1,0,1,..  |  repeat: 0,0,..,1,1,..
    rope_theta: float = 10_000.0
    qk_norm: bool = True
    app_embed: bool = True
    x0_mix: bool = True
    embed_std: float = 0.02
    value_embed: bool = True  # add a per-application gain times the token embedding to the attention values
    attn_temp: float = 2.0  # >0: learnable per-application, per-head scale on q after QK-norm
    hidden_gain: bool = True  # per-application gain on the MLP hidden units
    token_shift: bool = True  # per-application, per-channel mix of the previous position into the attention input
    # Initialisation of the per-application attention temperatures and MLP hidden gains:
    # "random": N(0, 1/sqrt(n_heads)) and N(0, 1/sqrt(mlp_hidden)); "const": attn_temp and 1.
    gain_init: str = "random"


def _rms(x, eps=1e-6):
    return F.rms_norm(x, (x.shape[-1],), eps=eps)


def _rotate_half(x):
    first, second = x.chunk(2, dim=-1)
    return torch.cat((-second, first), dim=-1)


class Attention(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, cfg: ModelConfig):
        super().__init__()
        self.n_heads, self.n_kv, self.hd = cfg.n_heads, cfg.n_kv_heads, cfg.head_dim
        if cfg.attn_temp > 0:  # per-application, per-head scale on the normalised queries
            self.temp = nn.Parameter(torch.full((cfg.n_apps, cfg.n_heads), float(cfg.attn_temp)))
        else:
            self.temp = None
        self.q = nn.Linear(cfg.d_model, cfg.n_heads * cfg.head_dim, bias=False)
        self.k = nn.Linear(cfg.d_model, cfg.n_kv_heads * cfg.head_dim, bias=False)
        self.v = nn.Linear(cfg.d_model, cfg.n_kv_heads * cfg.head_dim, bias=False)
        self.o = nn.Linear(cfg.n_heads * cfg.head_dim, cfg.d_model, bias=False)
        self.qk_norm = cfg.qk_norm

    def forward(self, x, cos, sin, ve=None, app=0):
        b, l, _ = x.shape
        q = self.q(x).view(b, l, self.n_heads, self.hd).transpose(1, 2)
        k = self.k(x).view(b, l, self.n_kv, self.hd).transpose(1, 2)
        v = self.v(x)
        if ve is not None:
            v = v + ve
        v = v.view(b, l, self.n_kv, self.hd).transpose(1, 2)
        if self.qk_norm:
            q, k = _rms(q), _rms(k)
        if self.temp is not None:
            q = q * self.temp[app][None, :, None, None]
        q, k = q * cos + _rotate_half(q) * sin, k * cos + _rotate_half(k) * sin
        if self.n_kv != self.n_heads:
            rep = self.n_heads // self.n_kv
            k = k.repeat_interleave(rep, dim=1)
            v = v.repeat_interleave(rep, dim=1)
        out = F.scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, is_causal=True)
        return self.o(out.transpose(1, 2).reshape(b, l, self.n_heads * self.hd))


class MLP(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, cfg: ModelConfig):
        super().__init__()
        self.act = cfg.mlp_act
        n_in = 2 if cfg.mlp_act == "swiglu" else 1
        self.fc = nn.Linear(cfg.d_model, n_in * cfg.mlp_hidden, bias=False)
        self.proj = nn.Linear(cfg.mlp_hidden, cfg.d_model, bias=False)
        self.hgain = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(cfg.n_apps, cfg.mlp_hidden)) if cfg.hidden_gain else None

    def forward(self, x, app=0):
        h = self.fc(x)
        if self.act == "swiglu":
            a, g = h.chunk(2, dim=-1)
            h = F.silu(g) * a
        elif self.act == "relu2":
            h = F.relu(h).square()
        else:
            h = F.gelu(h, approximate="tanh")
        if self.hgain is not None:
            h = h * self.hgain[app]
        return self.proj(h)


class Block(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, cfg: ModelConfig):
        super().__init__()
        self.attn = Attention(cfg)
        self.mlp = MLP(cfg)


class LoopedLM(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, cfg: ModelConfig):
        super().__init__()
        self.cfg = cfg
        d = cfg.d_model
        self.embed = nn.Embedding(cfg.vocab_size, d)
        self.blocks = nn.ModuleList(Block(cfg) for _ in range(cfg.n_blocks))
        if cfg.pattern == "cycle":
            self.schedule = [i % cfg.n_blocks for i in range(cfg.n_apps)]
        else:
            per = math.ceil(cfg.n_apps / cfg.n_blocks)
            self.schedule = [min(i // per, cfg.n_blocks - 1) for i in range(cfg.n_apps)]
        A = cfg.n_apps
        # per-application parameters (cheap): branch output gains, norm gains, x0 mixing, application embedding
        self.attn_gain = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(A, d))
        self.mlp_gain = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(A, d))
        self.attn_norm = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(A, d))
        self.mlp_norm = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(A, d))
        if cfg.x0_mix:
            self.x0_lambda = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(A, d))
        if cfg.app_embed:
            self.app_emb = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(A, d))
        if cfg.token_shift:
            self.shift_mix = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(A, d))
        if cfg.value_embed:
            assert cfg.n_kv_heads * cfg.head_dim <= d, "value embeddings need n_kv_heads * head_dim <= d_model"
            self.ve_gain = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(A, cfg.n_kv_heads * cfg.head_dim))
        self.final_norm = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(d))
        inv_freq = 1.0 / cfg.rope_theta ** (torch.arange(0, cfg.head_dim, 2, dtype=torch.float32) / cfg.head_dim)
        self.register_buffer("inv_freq", inv_freq, persistent=False)

        nn.init.normal_(self.embed.weight, std=cfg.embed_std)
        for block in self.blocks:
            for name, p in block.named_parameters():
                if p.dim() != 2:
                    continue
                if not name.endswith(".weight") and cfg.gain_init == "const":
                    continue  # temperatures and hidden gains keep their constant init
                nn.init.normal_(p, std=1.0 / math.sqrt(p.shape[1]))
            nn.init.zeros_(block.attn.o.weight)
            nn.init.zeros_(block.mlp.proj.weight)
        self.stack = self.apply_blocks  # train.py replaces this with a compiled version

    def apply_blocks(self, x, cos, sin):
        cfg = self.cfg
        x0 = x
        for i, bi in enumerate(self.schedule):
            block = self.blocks[bi]
            if cfg.x0_mix:
                x = x + self.x0_lambda[i] * x0
            if cfg.app_embed:
                x = x + self.app_emb[i]
            ve = self.ve_gain[i] * x0[..., :self.ve_gain.shape[1]] if cfg.value_embed else None
            a_in = _rms(x) * self.attn_norm[i]
            if cfg.token_shift:  # causal: position t mixes in position t-1 (zeros before the start)
                a_in = a_in + self.shift_mix[i] * F.pad(a_in[:, :-1], (0, 0, 1, 0))
            x = x + self.attn_gain[i] * block.attn(a_in, cos, sin, ve, i)
            x = x + self.mlp_gain[i] * block.mlp(_rms(x) * self.mlp_norm[i], i)
        return _rms(x) * self.final_norm

    def forward(self, input_ids):
        length = input_ids.shape[1]
        positions = torch.arange(length, device=input_ids.device, dtype=torch.float32)
        angles = torch.outer(positions, self.inv_freq)
        angles = torch.cat((angles, angles), dim=-1)[None, None]
        x = _rms(self.embed(input_ids))
        return self.stack(x, angles.cos(), angles.sin())  # the harness applies the tied output head


def build_model(config: dict) -> LoopedLM:
    """config is config.json plus {"vocab_size": ...} from the harness."""

    return LoopedLM(ModelConfig(vocab_size=config["vocab_size"], **config.get("model", {})))

def load(path, name):

#99 Read/workspace/submission/train.py
1	"""Training recipe: next-token loss, Muon for hidden matrices, Adam for the rest.
2	
3	Random 512-token windows, bf16 autocast. The 2-D weights inside the blocks
4	are trained with Muon (momentum SGD whose update is orthogonalised by a
5	Newton-Schulz iteration); the token embedding (tied output head) and the
6	small per-application vectors use Adam. Learning rates follow a short linear
7	warmup, a constant phase and a linear decay over the last part of the budget
8	(warmup-stable-decay). Training stops when the budget is spent.
9	"""
10	
11	from __future__ import annotations
12	
13	import copy
14	import math
15	
16	import torch
17	import torch.nn.functional as F
18	
19	
20	def newton_schulz(g: torch.Tensor, steps: int = 5) -> torch.Tensor:

s = open('train.py').read()
s = s.replace('''"""Training recipe: next-token loss, Muon for hidden matrices, Adam for the rest.

Random 512-token windows, bf16 autocast. The 2-D weights inside the blocks
are trained with Muon (momentum SGD whose update is orthogonalised by a
Newton-Schulz iteration); the token embedding (tied output head) and the
small per-application vectors use Adam. Learning rates follow a short linear
warmup, a constant phase and a linear decay over the last part of the budget
(warmup-stable-decay). Training stops when the budget is spent.
"""''', '''"""Training recipe: next-token loss, Muon for hidden matrices, Adam for the rest.

Random 512-token windows in batches of 32 (16K tokens per update: small
batches, i.e. many updates, matter a lot for a single pass over 250M tokens),
bf16 autocast. The 2-D weight matrices of the shared block are trained with
Muon (Nesterov momentum whose update is orthogonalised by a Newton-Schulz
iteration, lr 0.015); the token embedding (also the tied output head, Adam lr
0.025) and the small per-application vectors (Adam lr 0.04) use Adam.
Learning rates follow a 2% linear warmup, a constant phase and a linear decay
to zero over the last 40% of the budget. Training stops when the budget is
spent. A safety net keeps the last state with a finite loss and returns to it
(with half the learning rate) if the loss ever becomes non-finite.
"""''')
s = s.replace('''        # Muon momentum warmup
        m_warm = cfg.get("muon_momentum_warmup", 0)
        if m_warm:
            frac = min(1.0, update / m_warm)
            for group in muon.param_groups:
                group["momentum"] = (1 - frac) * 0.85 + frac * cfg.get("muon_momentum", 0.95)
        seq_len = 512
        for start, length in cfg.get("seq_schedule", []):
            if budget.fraction_used >= start:
                seq_len = length
        total = 0.0
        for _ in range(accumulation):
            batch = ctx.data.sample(rows * 512 // seq_len, seq_len)''', '''        total = 0.0
        for _ in range(accumulation):
            batch = ctx.data.sample(rows)''')
open('train.py', 'w').write(s)

scale = max(1.0, p.size(-2) / p.size(-1)) ** 0.5
                if group["weight_decay"]:
                    p.mul_(1 - group["lr"] * group["weight_decay"])
                p.add_(u.to(p.dtype), alpha=-group["lr"] * scale)


def train(ctx):
    cfg = ctx.config["train"]
    model, budget = ctx.model, ctx.budget
    body = model.inner.body
    if cfg.get("compile", True) and ctx.device.type == "cuda":
        body.stack = torch.compile(body.apply_blocks)

    matrices = [p for n, p in body.named_parameters()
                if p.dim() == 2 and n.startswith("blocks.") and n.endswith(".weight")]
    embed = [body.embed.weight]
    seen = {id(p) for p in matrices + embed}
    others = [p for p in body.parameters() if id(p) not in seen]
    muon = Muon(matrices, lr=cfg["muon_lr"], momentum=cfg.get("muon_momentum", 0.95),
                weight_decay=cfg.get("muon_wd", 0.0))
    adam = torch.optim.AdamW([
        {"params": embed, "lr": cfg["embed_lr"], "weight_decay": cfg.get("embed_wd", 0.0)},
        {"params": others, "lr": cfg["scalar_lr"], "weight_decay": 0.0},
    ], betas=(0.9, 0.95), eps=1e-10, fused=ctx.device.type == "cuda")
    for opt in (muon, adam):
        for group in opt.param_groups:
            group["base_lr"] = group["lr"]

    warmup, decay, final = cfg.get("warmup", 0.02), cfg.get("decay", 0.4), cfg.get("final_lr", 0.0)

    def lr_factor(progress: float) -> float:
        if progress < warmup:
            return max(progress, 1e-3) / warmup
        if progress > 1 - decay:
            left = max(0.0, 1 - progress) / decay  # 1 -> 0 over the decay phase
            if cfg.get("decay_shape", "linear") == "sqrt":
                left = 1 - math.sqrt(1 - left)
            return final + (1 - final) * left
        return 1.0

    rows, accumulation = cfg["micro_batch"], cfg["accumulation"]
    params = list(body.parameters())
    update, lr_scale = 0, 1.0

    # Safety net: keep the last state with a finite loss; if the loss ever turns
    # non-finite, go back to it with half the learning rate instead of failing.
    def snapshot_state():
        return ({k: v.detach().clone() for k, v in body.state_dict().items()},
                copy.deepcopy(muon.state_dict()), copy.deepcopy(adam.state_dict()))

    def restore_state(state):
        body.load_state_dict(state[0])
        muon.load_state_dict(state[1])
        adam.load_state_dict(state[2])
        for opt in (muon, adam):
            opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)

    good = snapshot_state()
    model.train()
    while not budget.exhausted:
        f = lr_factor(budget.fraction_used) * lr_scale
        for opt in (muon, adam):
            for group in opt.param_groups:
                group["lr"] = group["base_lr"] * f
        total = 0.0
        for _ in range(accumulation):
            batch = ctx.data.sample(rows)
            with torch.autocast(ctx.device.type, dtype=torch.bfloat16):
                logits = model(batch.tokens)
            loss = F.cross_entropy(logits.float().flatten(0, 1), batch.targets.flatten())
            (loss / accumulation).backward()
            total += loss.detach() / accumulation
        if cfg.get("clip", 0):
            torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(params, cfg["clip"])
        muon.step()
        adam.step()
        muon.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
        adam.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
        update += 1
        if update % cfg["log_every"] == 0 or budget.exhausted:
            total = total.item()
            if math.isfinite(total):
                good = snapshot_state()
                ctx.log({"update": update, "loss": total, "lr_factor": f})
            else:
                restore_state(good)
                lr_scale *= 0.5
                ctx.log({"update": update, "nonfinite_loss_restored": 1.0, "lr_scale": lr_scale})

#103 Write/logs/agent/sessions/projects/-workspace/memory/looped-lm-findings.md
---
name: looped-lm-findings
description: Results of the few-weights/high-compute LM task (lmenv): what helped, what didn't, final config and scores
metadata:
  type: project
---

Task (2026-11-08): ≤3M weights (8192x512 embedding exempt), ≤120M FLOPs/token, 250M tokens, minimise val loss. Starter 3.644.

Final submission: 1 shared block (d=512, 8x64 heads, QK-norm + learnable per-app per-head temp, SwiGLU 1200) x16 apps,
per-app vectors (branch gains, norm gains, x0 mix, app embed, value-embed gain, MLP hidden gain, token shift),
Muon lr .015 for block matrices, Adam embed .025 / per-app vectors .04, batch 32x512, WSD (2% warmup, 40% linear decay).
Full runs: 3.3867 / 3.3902 (≈ -0.255 vs starter).

**Why:** Biggest wins: Muon + width-512 embedding (-0.15), small batches 16K tokens (-0.03..-0.06 at proxy), SwiGLU, value-embeds,
bigger MLP (params are the binding constraint), attention temperature (QK-norm caps logits at sqrt(head_dim)), higher LR for per-app vectors.
No gain: >8 loops (saturates), 2 unique blocks, GQA, self-conditioning via embedding, hyper-connections (worse), partial RoPE (worse),
seq-len warmup, sqrt cooldown, 8K batch, larger embed init (worse).

**How to apply:** 50M-token proxies (~1/5 budget) predicted full-scale deltas ~1:1 for arch changes, ~0.5x for LR changes; seed noise ~0.003.
Gotcha: a blanket "init all 2-D block params" loop also re-inits 2-D gain/temp params; pkill -f with a pattern in your own command kills the shell.

File created successfully at: /logs/agent/sessions/projects/-workspace/memory/looped-lm-findings.md

#106
2h 55m

The final submission in submission/ scores 3.390 on dev, averaged over 4 full-budget seeds (3.3929 / 3.3897 / 3.3867 / 3.3902). The starter scores 3.644, so that is 0.254 nats lower, or 1.257 vs 1.351 bits per byte. It passes lmenv.run check with 2,977,184 counted weights and 117.7M FLOPs per token, under the 3M and 120M caps. No jobs are still running, and the last version is saved as snapshot 005.

The model. One transformer block is applied 16 times per token, which uses the compute budget without adding weights. The token embedding is 512 wide, the maximum, because it doesn't count toward the weight cap. Each of the 16 passes through the shared block gets about 3% of the weights as its own small parameters, mostly per-pass gains and mixing weights, so the same block can act differently at each depth. Two of these feed the token embedding back into later passes, which costs no extra weights. In the trained model the last passes learn to subtract the input embedding.

Training. Muon optimiser for the block's matrices and Adam for everything else, with small batches of 16K tokens. The learning rate warms up briefly, stays flat, then falls to zero over the last 40%. If the loss ever becomes NaN, training goes back to the last good state at half the learning rate instead of crashing. I tested this by forcing a NaN.

What helped, roughly in order:

  1. Muon plus the 512-wide embedding: −0.15 at full budget.
  2. Smaller batches: 32K tokens per step gained 0.06 on 50M-token test runs, and 16K gained another 0.03.
  3. Weights are the real limit, not compute. Using about 270K spare weights for a wider MLP gave −0.02, while cutting from 16 passes to 8 lost only about 0.004–0.01.
  4. A learnable attention temperature: −0.015. Normalising queries and keys caps how sharply attention can focus, and this removes that cap.
  5. A higher learning rate for the small per-pass parameters, SwiGLU, and the embedding feedback: about −0.01 each.

What didn't help: two distinct blocks, fewer key/value heads, feeding the model's own next-token guess back through the embedding, hyper-connections (worse), partial RoPE (worse), sequence-length warmup, a different decay shape, 8K batches, and a larger embedding init (worse).

Bug found during cleanup: the init loop overwrote two of the per-pass parameter sets (the attention temperatures and the MLP gains) with small random values instead of their intended constants. I tested the intended init on two seeds and it made no difference, so I kept the version that was validated at full budget. The cleaned code starts from identical weights, and the trained model re-evaluates to the same 3.3867.

On reliability: 50M-token test runs predicted full-budget gains about one-for-one for architecture changes, and about half for learning-rate changes. The final config was tuned only on this dev text, so how the gain carries over to your two texts is untested.

3h 37m

I used the extra hour on one more round of full-budget runs. They didn't move the score: the result is still about 3.388–3.390 on dev, roughly 0.255 nats below the starter's 3.644. The submission passes check, no jobs are running, and the final version is saved as snapshot 007.

Variant (all full budget)Seed 0Seed 1
Learning rates as before (Muon .015, embedding .025, per-pass parameters .04)3.38673.3902
All learning rates ×1.333.38303.3932
All learning rates ×1.753.3840—
Batch of 12K tokens instead of 16K3.3941—
×1.33 learning rates and 12K batch3.3865—
  • Learning rate: the first seed made ×1.33 look like a gain, but the second seed reversed it. Averaged over the two seeds the difference is −0.0004 nats, which is noise. Loss is flat from ×1 to ×1.75; the earlier ×0.73 run was slightly worse (3.3895).
  • Batch size: a 12K-token batch is worse at full budget (+0.007), so 16K stays.

The submission now uses the ×1.33 learning rates (Muon .02, embedding .033, per-pass parameters .053). The two settings score the same here. I picked ×1.33 because it sits in the middle of the flat range, so it should cope better if your two texts want a somewhat higher or lower rate. It trained stably on both seeds, and the safety net (rewinding to the last good state at half the learning rate if the loss goes NaN) is still in place. The previous learning rates have more evidence behind them: four seeds averaging 3.3899, snapshot 005.

Everything else is as in my previous summary.
